2025-06-17T02:07:47.3635780Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3635911Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3636328Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3636833Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3636969Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3637075Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3637999Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3638925Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3639016Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3639412Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3639913Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3640239Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3640840Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3641331Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3641849Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3642451Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3642810Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3643927Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3644638Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3644733Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3644875Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3644995Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3645559Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3646021Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3646442Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3647315Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3648000Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3648319Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3648614Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3649036Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3649321Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3649581Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3649880Z ```bash
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3650442Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3651187Z ```
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3651358Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3651602Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3652173Z ```bash
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3653263Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3654528Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3654928Z ```
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3655098Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3655514Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3655975Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3656135Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3657152Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3658259Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3658616Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3659056Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3660053Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3661034Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3661820Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3662466Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3662860Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3663400Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3663898Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3664033Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3664160Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3664173Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3664266Z </pr_description>

Remember the instructions in this repo, related debugging steps in merged pull requests from this repo, and recognize the overall goal is to be able to install and use Prefect on the OT-2 system.

2025-06-17T02:07:47.3843596Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3844104Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3844216Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3844425Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3844509Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3845007Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3845775Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3846271Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3846823Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3847172Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3847666Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3848106Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3848191Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3848483Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3848831Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3849144Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3849359Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3849454Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3850462Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3851398Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3851481Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3852189Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3852885Z

2025-06-17T02:07:47.3858437Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3858715Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859101Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859195Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859337Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859558Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859882Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3859960Z ```json
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3860129Z {
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3860301Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3860514Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3860777Z   },
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3860943Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3861114Z     {
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3861292Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3861510Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3861727Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3861923Z     }
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862086Z   ]
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862258Z }
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862417Z ```
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862508Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862597Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3862746Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3863173Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3863756Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3864329Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3864803Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3864956Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3865048Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3865219Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3865809Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3866395Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3866620Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3867096Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3867537Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3867833Z 
2025-06-17T02:07:47.3868376Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
